# Geography Form 3, Form Three Geography: authored answers for review

Every answer fixed by a person, not computed. Part 1: the tables, conventions and rules that computed answers come from.
Part 2: every authored question template, with all its data (keys, statements, pairs, choices and feedback) and sources.
Regenerate with `npm run review:f3-geography`. Item IDs (TR-…) match `docs/teacher-review.md`.

## Part 1

Rules the computed answers use are listed in the subject’s library tests; every authored answer is in Part 2.

### `g3-1-repair` · Lesson 1 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect claim.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ oblate shape: a near-sphere slightly flattened at the poles.
- ✅ globe: a reduced model of Earth's whole shape.
- ✅ map projection: a method of representing a curved surface on a plane.
- ✅ supporting evidence: observations that help test an explanation.
- ❌ Earth is a perfectly flat circular plate.  
  _↳ The global evidence supports a near-spherical planet with slight polar flattening._
- ❌ A globe has no scale because it is round.  
  _↳ A globe is a scaled model, not the full-sized planet._
- ❌ Every flat world map preserves every shape, area and distance exactly.  
  _↳ Flattening a curved surface introduces trade-offs and distortion._
- ❌ One obstructed view disproves every independent observation.  
  _↳ Assess several lines of evidence and the conditions of each observation._


Three generated variants:

> Find the incorrect claim.

- ◻️ map projection: a method of representing a curved surface on a plane.  
  _↳ explanation: This statement is correct: map projection means a method of representing a curved surface on a plane. Look for a claim that conflicts with its definition._
- ✅ One obstructed view disproves every independent observation.  
  _↳ explanation: Assess several lines of evidence and the conditions of each observation._
- ◻️ globe: a reduced model of Earth's whole shape.  
  _↳ explanation: This statement is correct: globe means a reduced model of Earth's whole shape. Look for a claim that conflicts with its definition._
- ◻️ oblate shape: a near-sphere slightly flattened at the poles.  
  _↳ explanation: This statement is correct: oblate shape means a near-sphere slightly flattened at the poles. Look for a claim that conflicts with its definition._

### `g3-6-repair` · Lesson 6 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect claim.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ latitude reading: the north/south angle read from the parallel through a point.
- ✅ longitude reading: the east/west angle read from the meridian through a point.
- ✅ hemisphere label: the direction that completes an angular coordinate.
- ✅ coordinate order: latitude followed by longitude.
- ❌ Read longitude labels to obtain latitude.  
  _↳ Use the parallel's latitude label, not the meridian's longitude label._
- ❌ Read latitude labels to obtain longitude.  
  _↳ Use the meridian's longitude label, not the parallel's latitude label._

  _↳ Following bedding is characteristic of a sill; cutting across is a dyke._

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A diagram marks where fault movement releases energy below ground.
Which explanation justifies the classification? | The origin of an earthquake within earth. | The surface point directly above the focus. · The observed shaking effects at a particular place. · Prepared behaviour reducing exposure to falling objects during shaking. | That explanation describes epicentre, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example. · That explanation describes intensity, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example. · That explanation describes protective action, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example. | The surface point above the focus is the epicentre. |
| A map marks the position above the underground origin.
Which explanation justifies the classification? | The surface point directly above the focus. | The origin of an earthquake within earth. · The observed shaking effects at a particular place. · Prepared behaviour reducing exposure to falling objects during shaking. | That explanation describes focus, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example. · That explanation describes intensity, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example. · That explanation describes protective action, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example. | Epicentre is a surface location, not a core depth. |
| Two towns report different damage from the same event.
Which explanation justifies the classification? | The observed shaking effects at a particular place. | The origin of an earthquake within earth. · The surface point directly above the focus. · Prepared behaviour reducing exposure to falling objects during shaking. | That explanation describes focus, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example. · That explanation describes epicentre, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example. · That explanation describes protective action, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example. | Local ground, distance and buildings can change observed intensity. |
| An indoor pupil takes nearby sturdy cover, protects the head and holds on away from windows.
Which explanation justifies the classification? | Prepared behaviour reducing exposure to falling objects during shaking. | The origin of an earthquake within earth. · The surface point directly above the focus. · The observed shaking effects at a particular place. | That explanation describes focus, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example. · That explanation describes epicentre, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example. · That explanation describes intensity, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example. | USGS guidance advises staying indoors during shaking and using Drop, Cover and Hold On rather than rushing outside. |

Right answer: `{X.r}`; wrong choices: `{X.w[0]}`, `{X.w[1]}`, `{X.w[2]}`

- Feedback `w0`: “{X.e[0]}”
- Feedback `w1`: “{X.e[1]}”
- Feedback `w2`: “{X.e[2]}”

Three generated variants:

> A map marks the position above the underground origin.
> Which explanation justifies the classification?

- ◻️ The observed shaking effects at a particular place.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That explanation describes intensity, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example._
- ◻️ Prepared behaviour reducing exposure to falling objects during shaking.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That explanation describes protective action, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example._
- ✅ The surface point directly above the focus.
- ◻️ The origin of an earthquake within earth.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That explanation describes focus, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example._

> Epicentre is a surface location, not a core depth.  
> So the answer is “The surface point directly above the focus.”.  

> A diagram marks where fault movement releases energy below ground.
> Which explanation justifies the classification?

- ◻️ The observed shaking effects at a particular place.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That explanation describes intensity, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example._
- ◻️ Prepared behaviour reducing exposure to falling objects during shaking.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That explanation describes protective action, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example._
- ✅ The origin of an earthquake within earth.
- ◻️ The surface point directly above the focus.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That explanation describes epicentre, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example._

Full working after a second miss:

> The surface point above the focus is the epicentre.  
> So the answer is “The origin of an earthquake within earth.”.  

> An indoor pupil takes nearby sturdy cover, protects the head and holds on away from windows.
> Which explanation justifies the classification?

- ✅ Prepared behaviour reducing exposure to falling objects during shaking.
- ◻️ The origin of an earthquake within earth.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That explanation describes focus, rather than the relationship illustrated in this example._
- ◻️ The surface point directly above the focus.

Sources: earth (NASA Earth facts/seasons; NOAA plate tectonics and Earth layers; USGS earthquake protection; standard school geographical coordinates and ideal local-time calculations)

### `g3-17-repair` · Lesson 17 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect claim.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ focus: the origin of an earthquake within Earth.
- ✅ epicentre: the surface point directly above the focus.
- ✅ intensity: the observed shaking effects at a particular place.
- ✅ protective action: prepared behaviour reducing exposure to falling objects during shaking.
- ❌ The focus is always the point directly above the earthquake on the surface.  
  _↳ The surface point above the focus is the epicentre._
- ❌ The epicentre is necessarily the deepest point inside the core.  
  _↳ Epicentre is a surface location, not a core depth._
- ❌ One event must produce identical observed effects in every town.  
  _↳ Local ground, distance and buildings can change observed intensity._
- ❌ Running down stairs during shaking is the recommended first indoor action.  
  _↳ USGS guidance advises staying indoors during shaking and using Drop, Cover and Hold On rather than rushing outside._


Three generated variants:

> Find the incorrect claim.

- ◻️ protective action: prepared behaviour reducing exposure to falling objects during shaking.  
  _↳ explanation: This statement is correct: protective action means prepared behaviour reducing exposure to falling objects during shaking. Look for a claim that conflicts with its definition._
- ◻️ focus: the origin of an earthquake within Earth.  
  _↳ explanation: This statement is correct: focus means the origin of an earthquake within Earth. Look for a claim that conflicts with its definition._
- ✅ One event must produce identical observed effects in every town.  
  _↳ explanation: Local ground, distance and buildings can change observed intensity._
- ◻️ intensity: the observed shaking effects at a particular place.  
  _↳ explanation: This statement is correct: intensity means the observed shaking effects at a particular place. Look for a claim that conflicts with its definition._

Full working after a second miss:

> The wrong statement is “One event must produce identical observed effects in every town.”.  
> Local ground, distance and buildings can change observed intensity.

- ✅ The epicentre is necessarily the deepest point inside the core.  
  _↳ explanation: Epicentre is a surface location, not a core depth._
- ◻️ epicentre: the surface point directly above the focus.  
  _↳ explanation: This statement is correct: epicentre means the surface point directly above the focus. Look for a claim that conflicts with its definition._
- ◻️ focus: the origin of an earthquake within Earth.  
  _↳ explanation: This statement is correct: focus means the origin of an earthquake within Earth. Look for a claim that conflicts with its definition._

Full working after a second miss:

> The wrong statement is “The epicentre is necessarily the deepest point inside the core.”.  
> Epicentre is a surface location, not a core depth.  

> Find the incorrect claim.

- ✅ The epicentre is necessarily the deepest point inside the core.  
  _↳ explanation: Epicentre is a surface location, not a core depth._
